I suppose that’s what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take as much pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, activities, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. Lately I’ll get the bag ready and leave it by the door, and then I just… don’t go. It’s not that I dislike them, exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough in the moment. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low in mood for most of the day, or does it come and go more randomly?\n\nPatient: Most days, I think… especially in the mornings. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It may lift a little here and there, so I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, it’s there most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual or that you’re lacking energy, even if you’ve had enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that clearly, no. I mean, I do move a bit slower some days, but at my age I don’t always know what counts as “more tired than usual.” I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s just ordinary tiredness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re not worth much, or that you’ve let people down, even if you don’t have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I mean… I have moments where I think I could be doing better, or I feel a little guilty for not keeping up with things, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless, no. At least, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Sometimes I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind just feels foggy. Then I end up not doing either for a while. I suppose that counts as trouble focusing, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your lack of interest in activities, like your watercolor class or book club, has affected your daily life or how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit. I’ve stopped teaching my little craft classes, which I used to be quite good about keeping up with. And I’ve missed some calls and appointments, and little family things I used to handle carefully.\n\nI’m still managing at home, I don’t want you to think I’m not. But I do rely more on reminders now, and I put things off that used to just be routine. I guess that does count as affecting how I function, doesn’t it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But lately it’s been happening enough that I noticed—like I’ll completely miss lunch without meaning to, and then another day I’m just nibbling at crackers or sweets at the counter. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’ll catch myself standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right. Some days I just forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing really tastes right. I thought maybe I should come in and mention it… if that’s alright.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on? Has it been happening every day, or are there days when you feel more normal about eating?\n\nPatient: It’s not every single day, no… at least I don’t think so. There are some days that feel more normal. But lately it’s been happening enough that I noticed—like I’ll completely miss lunch without meaning to, and then another day I’m just nibbling at crackers or sweets at the counter. It feels a little silly to make much of it, but my eating has just gotten sort of turned around.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating sweets or crackers at the counter, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or that you’re losing control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I can’t stop, no… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more that my eating feels all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’ll catch myself standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right. I suppose that’s what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take as much pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, activities, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. Lately I’ll get the bag ready and leave it by the door, and then I just… don’t go. It’s not that I dislike them, exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough in the moment. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low in mood for most of the day, or does it come and go more randomly?\n\nPatient: Most days, I think… especially in the mornings. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed. I mean, I do move a bit slower some days, but at my age I don’t always know what counts as “more tired than usual.” I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s just ordinary tiredness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re not worth much, or that you’ve let people down, even if you don’t have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I mean… I have moments where I think I could be doing better, or I feel a little guilty for not keeping up with things, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless, no. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F50.01, F50.81, F50.89\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in today? How can I help you?\n\nPatient: Well… it’s probably not the biggest thing, but my eating has been a bit off lately. Some days I just forget lunch altogether, and then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing really tastes right. I thought maybe I should come in and mention it… if that’s alright.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on? Has it been happening every day, or are there days when you feel more normal about eating?\n\nPatient: It’s not every single day, no… at least I don’t think so. There are some days that feel more normal. But lately it’s been happening enough that I noticed—like I’ll completely miss lunch without meaning to, and then another day I’m just nibbling at crackers or sweets at the counter. It feels a little silly to make much of it, but my eating has just gotten sort of turned around.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating sweets or crackers at the counter, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or that you’re losing control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I can’t stop, no… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more that my eating feels all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’ll catch myself standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right. I suppose that’s what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take as much pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, activities, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. Lately I’ll get the bag ready and leave it by the door, and then I just… don’t go. It’s not that I dislike them, exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough in the moment. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low in mood for most of the day, or does it come and go more randomly?\n\nPatient: Most days, I think… especially in the mornings. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It may lift a little here and there, so I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, it’s there most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual or that you’re lacking energy, even if you’ve had enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that clearly, no. I mean, I do move a bit slower some days, but at my age I don’t always know what counts as “more tired than usual.” I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s just ordinary tiredness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re not worth much, or that you’ve let people down, even if you don’t have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I mean… I have moments where I think I could be doing better, or I feel a little guilty for not keeping up with things, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless, no. At least, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Sometimes I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind just feels foggy. Then I end up not doing either for a while. I suppose that counts as trouble focusing, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your lack of interest in activities, like your watercolor class or book club, has affected your daily life or how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit. I’ve stopped teaching my little craft classes, which I used to be quite good about keeping up with. And I’ve missed some calls and appointments, and little family things I used to handle carefully.\n\nI’m still managing at home, I don’t want you to think I’m not. But I do rely more on reminders now, and I put things off that used to just be routine. I guess that does count as affecting how I function, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but my eating has gotten sort of turned around. Some days I realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, and then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing really tastes right.\n\nI haven’t been trying to change my weight or anything. It’s just… not very regular lately. Is that the sort of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or concentrate on tasks, like reading or following a conversation, more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though maybe not terribly. It’s more like I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind gets foggy. Then I end up doing neither for a while.\n\nI don’t know if that’s what you mean by concentration, but it does feel harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been experiencing a loss of energy or feeling fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been physically active?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say nearly every day, no. I mean, at my age I do get tired, of course, but I haven’t really been thinking of it as a big loss of energy or anything like that.\n\nSome days I move slower than I used to, but that may just be ordinary. I do have slower days, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nMaybe I’m just a little worn out sometimes, but nothing I’ve been keeping track of as a real problem. Has it been happening every day, or are there days when you feel more normal about eating?\n\nPatient: It’s not every single day, no… at least I don’t think so. There are some days that feel more normal. But lately it’s been happening enough that I noticed—like I’ll completely miss lunch without meaning to, and then another day I’m just nibbling at crackers or sweets at the counter. It feels a little silly to make much of it, but my eating has just gotten sort of turned around.\n\nDoctor: When you find yourself eating sweets or crackers at the counter, does it ever feel like you can’t stop or that you’re losing control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like I can’t stop, no… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more that my eating feels all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’ll catch myself standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right. I suppose that’s what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take as much pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, activities, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. Lately I’ll get the bag ready and leave it by the door, and then I just… don’t go. It’s not that I dislike them, exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough in the moment. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low in mood for most of the day, or does it come and go more randomly?\n\nPatient: Most days, I think… especially in the mornings. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It may lift a little here and there, so I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, it’s there most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual or that you’re lacking energy, even if you’ve had enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that clearly, no. I mean, I do move a bit slower some days, but at my age I don’t always know what counts as “more tired than usual.” I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s just ordinary tiredness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re not worth much, or that you’ve let people down, even if you don’t have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I mean… I have moments where I think I could be doing better, or I feel a little guilty for not keeping up with things, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless, no. At least, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But I do rely more on reminders now, and I put things off that used to just be routine. I guess that does count as affecting how I function, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but my eating has gotten sort of turned around. It’s just… not very regular lately. Then I end up doing neither for a while.\n\nI don’t know if that’s what you mean by concentration, but it does feel harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been experiencing a loss of energy or feeling fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been physically active?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say nearly every day, no. It’s more that my eating feels all turned around. Some days I forget lunch completely, and then other days I’ll catch myself standing there eating crackers or sweets, even though they don’t really taste right. I suppose that’s what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, depressed, or have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but most mornings I wake up with this heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I’ve even gotten dressed. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t take as much pleasure in the things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, activities, or time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I used to really look forward to my watercolor class, and the quilting circle, and book club too. Lately I’ll get the bag ready and leave it by the door, and then I just… don’t go. It’s not that I dislike them, exactly. I just can’t seem to make myself care enough in the moment. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean, but it’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel down or low in mood for most of the day, or does it come and go more randomly?\n\nPatient: Most days, I think… especially in the mornings. I wake up with that heavy, low feeling, like the color’s already gone out of the day before I even get dressed. It may lift a little here and there, so I don’t want to overstate it, but yes, it’s there most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you feel more tired than usual or that you’re lacking energy, even if you’ve had enough sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that clearly, no. I mean, I do move a bit slower some days, but at my age I don’t always know what counts as “more tired than usual.” I wouldn’t want to say yes if it’s just ordinary tiredness, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you’re not worth much, or that you’ve let people down, even if you don’t have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d put it that strongly. I mean… I have moments where I think I could be doing better, or I feel a little guilty for not keeping up with things, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m worthless, no. At least, I don’t think so. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to think clearly, focus, or make decisions lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Sometimes I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind just feels foggy. Then I end up not doing either for a while. I suppose that counts as trouble focusing, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your lack of interest in activities, like your watercolor class or book club, has affected your daily life or how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose it has, more than I’d like to admit. I’ve stopped teaching my little craft classes, which I used to be quite good about keeping up with. And I’ve missed some calls and appointments, and little family things I used to handle carefully.\n\nI’m still managing at home, I don’t want you to think I’m not. But I do rely more on reminders now, and I put things off that used to just be routine. I guess that does count as affecting how I function, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite or weight recently, like eating much less or more than usual, or losing or gaining weight without trying?\n\nPatient: Yes… a bit. I don’t think it’s anything dramatic, but my eating has gotten sort of turned around. Some days I realize I’ve forgotten lunch completely, and then other days I’ll be standing at the counter eating crackers or sweets, even though nothing really tastes right.\n\nI haven’t been trying to change my weight or anything. It’s just… not very regular lately. Is that the sort of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you found it harder to think clearly or concentrate on tasks, like reading or following a conversation, more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so, though maybe not terribly. It’s more like I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like whether I should call the pharmacy first or open the mail, and my mind gets foggy. Then I end up doing neither for a while.\n\nI don’t know if that’s what you mean by concentration, but it does feel harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you’ve been experiencing a loss of energy or feeling fatigued nearly every day, even when you haven’t been physically active?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say nearly every day, no. I mean, at my age I do get tired, of course, but I haven’t really been thinking of it as a big loss of energy or anything like that.\n\nSome days I move slower than I used to, but that may just be ordinary. I do have slower days, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nMaybe I’m just a little worn out sometimes, but nothing I’ve been keeping track of as a real problem. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling like you're not worth much, or that you've let people down, even if you don't have a clear reason for feeling that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a strong way. Is that the sort of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you've been moving more slowly than usual, or that you can't seem to sit still, more than you used to?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make too much of it, but I have noticed my body feels slower lately. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these changes in how quickly you move—like feeling slowed down or needing to pace—happen almost every day, or are they more occasional?\n\nPatient: I’d say… probably closer to almost every day, though not all day long.
